And at the same time, why right now and not why ever before? It is necessary to know that such a decision has already prepared a decision in the previous term to return the national Slovenian tourism organisation for a simple reason. The decision taken in 2012 – to merge the WTO into SPIRIT with the remaining two areas, namely JAPTI, is true entrepreneurship, foreign investment; and at the same time the TIA, according to the Public Agency for Technological Development, has been made at least two steps. One step was certainly austerity, for there was a period of saving in all areas. The second reason is because the incredible synergies were expected if all three areas were to be combined; and based on the combined content, there would be greater results. The fact is, in all this time from 2012 until now, we can only find out the following. Apart from the fact that nominally less has been spent on tourism, to promote tourism; some of the effects on the other hand with this merger have not really been. However, we have had the result that the Slovenian Tourism Organisation actually drowned in SPIRIT itself. In fact, we are one of the few countries that no longer had a national tourism organisation, which would in fact have one of the main tasks of all other areas, as are usually national tourism organisations, and this is the promotion of Slovenian tourism. Some had objected to this decision at the time, but the profession was not heard, and that was why it took place. I believe and hope that today we are actually correcting only this act with this disunity itself. It must be said that this is too little to do for tourism, but it can be a good start. Here, the ministry will have a lot of tasks, or ministry, with all the other steps that are still necessary and necessary – and I believe that they will also be fulfilled – can ensure the conditions necessary to give us the right to live; and above all, that we get the benefits from what we have from our natural heritage in the field of Slovenian tourism. Let me say that the decision to take this step in the association is, above all, to save time. Why the time? You know that the government was only formed in September and it was practically difficult to expect that the Slovenian government would be immediately the next day, given that this organisation is important mainly because it remains important that the Slovenian Tourism Organisation be operational in the beginning of the new year. I believe we have saved some time with this step. In particular, it is time for the Minister to be able, with other decisions and other policies that he will set out in this area; for it is also true that the Minister also provides them, so that we have not intervened in any way within the law itself, and in this way begins to create conditions. Three things are urgently needed to happen in the same law, so we have considered and proposed certain solutions within this law. One is certainly a tourist tax, which has also been extensively discussed on the committee itself. The decision has fallen to the effect that the tourist tax will not be rectified within this law. However, in mind also the Ministry and Members, when we are always afraid and trying to turn any change in the tourist tax into a new tax. Indeed, it is not a new tax; it is an existing tax, which, in fact, is always going back to tourism by law, which means that these are strictly the means of purpose for the same area that we are dealing with today. It should be noted that this is a minimal increase, especially since the tourist tax was last changed in 2003. Last year's correction was so minor that by means of these additional funds, which would have been received and would have been devoted to the field of tourism, it would have been possible to resolve a number of complex and resource-efficient solutions precisely by these means. Well, if not today; probably this year, in the future with the decision of the ministry. It is important to say that, if we are already establishing a separate Slovenian Tourism Organisation, it is also necessary to provide the basic means by which it will operate. It is too small to say that we need the WTO, and then we don't give them enough money on the other side. In his own explanation and in the debate on the Committee, the Minister said that one of the major steps that will be taken this year for the task in the WTO is to visit Expo, which means that we only need €2 million for this part. However, we have already set up substantially lower funds in the balance sheet and in some ways have not allowed the Ministry and the Ministry of MGRT to provide these funds in this way. In this way, he will have to fight for additional funds in such a way or other way, since these funds are now a little over 5 million too small for the WTO to start making other commitments. The second thing that seems important to me is that we also have regional destination organisations within this law. As we know, these were created at the time of the previous financial perspective when some funds were provided for the operation of these so-called RDOs. Since the financing of these RDOs is over and these RDOs have had certain effects, it is in some ways pointless to simply sink and stop them. That is why I expect this part to be eliminated with the amendment, because the Minister's words are that he will in some way try to ensure the future functioning of these RDOs; I hope that we will be able to hold him to his word and that in this way we will maintain this activity, which has also produced results in certain areas. In this way, we will overcome a variety of mayoral logic that only works up to one border, especially because RDOs are generally organised in the area of a large number of municipalities. And you know that every mayor looks to his limit, and only within those boundaries does he regulate, and he doesn't really care about everything else anymore. You know that the effects, especially in the field, simply cannot be confused with a line that applies only to Ljubljana, Save or Drava. What is important to us is that today, with a decision, I hope that even with a fruitful debate, we will finally get the WTO itself, which will finally bring us back tourism. Let me put it this way. Because it is a sector that is one of the five industries in this country that brings serious funds under GDP – 12%, that it is an area where more than 45 000 people are employed, and because it is also an area where huge private capital is invested with a single objective, for the first time, to produce new employment and to get resources, and also to offer our natural wealth that we have, I hope that this moment, if we do not reach for bigger, more courageous legs in the field of tourism; in fact, all that we are creating today, is somehow gifted and made by accident. Unfortunately, we can still see that in the area of tourism, even in the past, there is a lack of listening and some way of networking. The amendment of the SDS Group to Article 3 was then irrelevant, unfortunately. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on and adopted all the articles of the proposal. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the supplementary proposal, which includes all the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report. As regards the proposal for a law amending the Law on the Promotion of Foreign Direct Investment and the Internationalisation of Enterprises, it was also found on the Committee at the same meeting, in view of the opinion transmitted by the Legal and Legislative Service, which it examined in terms of compliance with the Constitution and the legal system, that this law should not be adopted before the amendment of the Law on the Promotion of Tourism. Members and members of the Committee did not discuss the individual articles of the bill. No amendments were tabled to the bill. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all articles of the bill and adopted them. ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u5	I give the floor to the representative of the Government for an introductory explanation of the opinion. Minister for Economic Development and Technology Mr Zdravko Restoršek, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u6	Mr President of the National Assembly, Members and Members of Parliament! Dear Member – the proposaler of the Troika of Laws! You have before you the proposal of three laws to amend the Law on the Promotion of the Development of Tourism, the Law on the Support Environment for Entrepreneurship and the Proposal for a Law amending the Law on the Promotion of Foreign Direct Investment and the Internationalisation of Enterprises. The novelties of all these three laws create a legal basis for defining and transferring the tourism tasks now managed by SPIRIT to a newly established public agency responsible for the marketing and promotion of tourism. In order to define and transfer the tourism tasks now carried out by the public agency SPIRIT Slovenia to the newly established public agency responsible for the marketing and promotion of tourism, our government or ministry, which supported this proposal by the proposer, is also taking part. Why? Because tourism is one of the five industries in which we intend to build the development of the Slovenian economy; because tourism today represents 13% of gross social product, it employs 45 000 people and creates a 2 billion euro inflow from foreign tourism. From this title, the state budget also has 250 million VAT collected each year because foreign tourists spend in Slovenia. It is fortunate that we have recognized the importance of tourism before time, but it is unfortunate that we have not followed this recognition by action. After a few years in this Parliament, the Strategy for the Development of Slovenian Tourism, which envisioned and still foresees an increase in foreign tourism flows from 2 to 3 billion euros on purely real grounds, things started to go in a negative direction. We halved the funds for promotion, followed by other reasons. Instead of 3 billion or at least a quarter of what we should have achieved this year, we are still in the same place, more or less we are holding onto these 2 billion. Here is one, not the only, but an important reason that we have abolished the Slovenian Tourism Organisation in this interim period, drowned it in SPIRIT, so to speak, and in some ways we have not achieved any of the intended synergies that were provided at the time in carrying out this act. In short, €3 billion inflows from foreign tourism also means an additional 10,000 people, but the potential for growth and development of Slovenian tourism is mainly in the foreign market. That is why we are obliged to do more to promote our country, which will benefit not only tourism but one of these five important industries for our country, but also all the other economies. What all countries in the world are finally doing, and if you want to in Europe, from 28 members currently have their own tourism organisation 26. I believe that at the end of today's 27th day, we will be a country that will re-establish its independent Slovenian tourism organisation. Of course, it must be said that the establishment of the Slovenian Tourism Organisation alone is not enough. If we want to launch and exploit all the potentials of Slovenian tourism, we will have to take several steps to continue. The one that is not related to this, but I will mention it all the same, is the internationalisation of Slovenian tourism, which we must carry out in two areas. Internationalisation in terms of more guests. We are today at 63% of foreign guests; we make a difference in traffic on the domestic market. As I said before, growth and development are possible in foreign markets. We have immeasurable potential in our immediate vicinity and this all relates back to the WTO. The second step of internationalisation also means internationalisation, however difficult this may sound, in the ownership sense. Indeed, there is no danger that we will reach not only foreign owners in the field of tourism, with which we will get even more in contact with the foreign market, new knowledge, technologies that will try to raise our tourism level to a higher level. The transition itself or the establishment of a new Slovenian Tourism Organisation must be said that this is the first step in waiting for us to see the things that my predecessors have mentioned. First of all, we need to stabilise the revenues of the Slovenian Tourism Organisation, which will be based not only on the budget, but on the inclusion of this issue. One of the important things we need to consider thoroughly is the tourist tax already mentioned. It is also about the participation of tourism companies. In short, we need to get to a system that will work and that will, in some long-term period, if I transfer things from today's relationship, allow, to some extent, 20 million a year, the state – and within it – to present tourism to the maximum extent. The Slovenian tourism organisation will take care of two issues, development in the field of Slovenian tourism and – in particular – promotion in foreign markets. On the inside, we will have to tackle the problem of the above-mentioned regional destination organisations and establish a system that is now operating at local level. We have relatively good more or less throughout the country-wide distributed local tourism organisations. We have some regional destination organisations that have taken advantage of their opportunity through funding and European funds. We've got more where these things didn't happen. We also have this SPIRIT conglomerate, which also includes the Slovenian Tourism Organisation. I believe that this new law will establish a pyramid that will work. The first two – a local tourist and regional destination organisation on the domestic market and a Slovenian tourist organisation mainly abroad. I believe that when we come to the establishment and through the adoption of all three laws, the first step towards tourism is to take an important step forward, to get closer to these 3 billion foreign tourism traffic and to justify our role as an important industry within five in the Slovenian economy. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u8	Honourable President, Honourable Minister with Colleagues, Honourable Colleagues and Colleagues! We are considering a package of laws whose proposed solutions are the definition and transfer of the tourism tasks now being carried out by the SPIRIT public agency to the newly established public agency responsible for the marketing and promotion of WTO tourism and related urgent changes. Social Democrats will therefore give a common position on all proposed laws. We support the proposed solutions in our parliamentary group as we were detained and had concerns when adopting amendments to the legislation that meant the inclusion of the WTO in the SPIRIT agency. These proved to be justified, since despite the expected effects, the desired results have not been achieved. As the proponents state, it soon became apparent that the changes were adopted and implemented without serious analysis, causing significant damage, particularly in terms of the visibility of Slovenia as a tourist destination. One agency brought together too diverse content and no expected synergies. Tourism contributes to the Slovenian economy close to 13% of Slovenian GDP, generates about 45 000 jobs and contributes 250 million euros a year to the state budget. Tourism is an economic sector, but it is multidisciplinary and involves practically all sectors of our country. Therefore, it is so important that this is done in an integrated manner by an independent body, one of the most important tasks of which will be the integration and coordination of all stakeholders. In order to strengthen the contribution of tourism to economic growth, a quality move and targeted action is needed, complemented by the necessary resources. A new systemic source of funding is needed, as the tourism sector must not depend solely on budgetary financing, which is under constant pressure from the austerity policy. The functioning of the independent public agency for the planning and implementation of the marketing and promotion policy of Slovenia's comprehensive tourism offer on domestic and foreign markets will, according to the applicants, contribute to the effective implementation of the marketing and promotion of Slovenian tourism and the creation of effective instruments to support these activities. We attach social democrats to this. Slovenia is a wanted tourist destination that attracts guests with its varied nature, with the richness of its historical, cultural and culinary heritage; many with the highest level of service and with unique opportunities for experiences. Tourism is an activity that not only ensures the achievement of economic objectives but also enables the placement of domestic food products, also enables the maintenance of a balanced population and employment and is an integral part of the promotion of our country. From this point of view, it is also important to develop agriculture, not only as an economic sector, but also as an activity that also co-creates the conditions for tourism, which can provide tourists with quality local and typical food. Taking into account the experience of the European Union, therefore, the awareness of advanced, technologically developed societies is returning to nature. Ecological production has also spread to organic agriculture, one of the most dynamic sectors of the European Union. This opened the market niche for farms in the Slovenian space, as Slovenia has an excellent opportunity with its natural resources to make good use of positive directions. We have written in the Tourism Strategy that tourism in Slovenia will be based on sustainable development and, as a very successful economic sector, will make a key contribution to the social well-being and reputation of our country in the world. A well-preserved nature and nature are key competitive advantages of Slovenia, from which comes the top story of Slovenia and the basic area of the tourist offer of Slovenia, which strengthens the brand of Slovenia and Slovenian tourism I feel Slovenia. Social Democrats are convinced that economic development is primarily linked to people and their active participation in the creation of prosperity. Successful economies are, in our view, capable of involving as many people as possible in their processes and at the same time employing all their creative potentials. The Social Democrats will, of course, support the proposals of all three new bills. Also because the proposed solution follows the commitments of the Coalition Treaty, where we have committed ourselves to the exclusion of the WTO from the SPIRIT public agency and have identified the WTO as a central national tourist promotion institution.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u15	The word is given by the SMC Group, in its name mag. Margareta Guček Zakošek.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u16	Thank you, Mr. President. All in attendance, all in attendance! Today we have before us the so-called Troika of Laws, which provides for the definition of tourism from the public agency SPIRIT Slovenia. The tasks are therefore transferred to the new public agency for the marketing and promotion of tourism or the independent Slovenian Tourism Organisation. I too will present my position under all three laws. We have heard a lot about how tourism is of particular importance to the Slovenian economy; and we agree with that. In 2012, the government adopted proposals for three pieces of legislation allowing for the transformation of institutions to promote competitiveness and development, entrepreneurship, tourism and internationalisation. TIA, JAPTI and the WTO thus merged into SPIRIT. We believe that a step has been taken in order to save money. The subsequent withdrawal of funds for the promotion of tourism has resulted in a final material, human and financial degradation of the WTO. In this case, too, we can learn, as in the case of many companies, what happens when we take money away from promotions at a time of crisis. At the time of the crisis, it comes back to us as a boomerang. We will achieve this by establishing an intolerable state and using a great deal of resources to restore the brand's visibility back. Today we are on a similar path, and we must be aware of that. Above all, it turned out that the expected synergies of the merger were not at all; on the contrary, there have been negative physical and recent financial indicators. It was therefore a matter of merging the various organisations into a platform of one organisational structure. The debate has often raised the question of what we will gain if the WTO is singled out. Can the organizational change itself really mean anything in the substantive and strategic sense of the organization? And we answer with the organization's theory — yes. Because we believe that's the key. It is precisely the organisational structure that many times defines only the functioning of the organisation; and the organisational structure is in fact the one that has to adapt to the content; and not the other way around, what happened in the case of the WTO merger. In the case of the WTO, which is a strong market organisation, which must maintain a high level of flexibility, creativity in thinking and communication, and highly extraverted towards its external and internal stakeholders, the organisational structure is the first important basis for good functioning. It also needs clear responsibility and expertise to manage the organisation, personnel and operations; as well as the strategic orientation of the same organisation and all persons within it with a vision of their own. It is the organisation that determines the relationship and relationship between key stakeholders at different levels of business. If we don't know where we're going and who the captain is, every way through the sea is useless. The government has therefore adopted an opinion on all three novels and supported them, as well as amendments to the proposal for a law on amendments and amendments to the Act on the Promotion of the Development of Tourism proposed by the ZaAB Group. In order to eliminate the professional and developmental tasks in the field of tourism as soon as possible from the work of SPIRIT, we will, of course, support all three legislative proposals in the SMC Group, including government proposals for amendments. Indeed, we do not agree with the proposed legal arrangements for regional destination organisations or RDOs and thus with the rise in the value of the tourist tax point. The SMC Group supports all amendments, as it seems to us to be the regulation of independent RDOs and to modify the tourist tax regime at this time premature and non-strategic. While RDOs were created on the basis of three years of funding for the promotion of cohesion funds regions, they had many positive signs. In particular, certain RDOs operated extremely well or operated, such as the RDO of the central Slovenian regions. Shareholders were linked and new active tourist products were created, but the project also had negative effects or views; for example, these RDOs were not tourist destinations and many new brands were created. On the other hand, as we have heard, we already have financial difficulties with the support of the basic national brand. Such a proposal also opens the door to a further increase in the number of RDOs, which is totally contrary to what the Government of the Republic of Slovenia has committed to – so to speak, the reduction of public institutions. Given the limited resources, it is therefore advisable to strengthen the national trade mark first, and this is also the opinion of the tourism economy itself. The systematic provision of resources and the functioning of the RDO would reduce already limited resources for the implementation of tourism activities or in the area of the development of Slovenian tourism. We also point out that the WTO's independence is just the beginning, this is the first step that needs to be followed as soon as possible by organisational and substantive consolidation and, last but not least, the provision of systemic resources. However, we believe that a strong national promotion organisation is key to the quality development of tourism, and we agree with the estimates that knowledge of Slovenian tourism offers abroad is currently poor. We have lost the strategic thread, so we need to highlight new, innovative market paths as soon as possible, but we can only create them within the WTO. The new WTO also shows the following areas in the analysis: the specialisation of the governance structure, the organisation of work processes, more flexible cooperation with all stakeholders, the preparation of a independent long-term vision, easier transformation of additional resources and the establishment of a programme council and more active policies; and, above all, greater responsiveness of organisational units to current events. Establish new agencies in terms of the costs associated with the establishment does not currently entail additional financial burdens for the budget. In the SMC Group, we believe that the WTO, as a single national tourism organisation, will carry out its mission more effectively, such as good planning, quality marketing and promotion of Slovenia's comprehensive tourist offer in domestic and foreign markets. It is important that the strategy of Slovenia and the vision of the development of Slovenia and the functioning and vision of the WTO go hand in hand. We believe that this objective will be better achieved on the WTO's own path and that Slovenian tourism will become one of Slovenia's major economic development sectors in the 21st century. In this way, the WTO will be able to deal more actively with content alone and thus support all stakeholders in the economy. Let the world with us finally "feel" Slovenia.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u37	Thank you very much for your word, Madam President, and for your apology for the transfer. The report to the Proposal for a Law amending and supplementing the Law on Explosives and pyrotechnic articles – the Committee on the Interior, Public Administration and Local Self-Government is held at 6th ordinary session 12. 2. 2015 as a parent working body, considered the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Explosives and pyrotechnic articles submitted to the National Assembly for consideration by the Government. The amendments were tabled by the SDS MPs and the SMC, Desus and SD MPs, which are 12. 2. In 2015, amendments to the amendments to Articles 2, 6 and 16 of the Law were tabled. The representative of the proposer – the State Secretary at the Ministry of the Interior presented, in a supplementary explanation, the main solutions to the draft law, which aims to bring the two European directives into the Slovenian legal order. Regarding the amendments tabled by the coalition parliamentary groups, he said that they were supported by the proposer as they followed the comments of the Legal and Legislative Service and were mostly of a nomotechnic nature. With regard to the amendments of the SDS Group, which extends the prohibition on the sale, possession and use of fireworks to category 1 fireworks, he said that such a provision would be contrary to the Directive. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service said in her presentation that the comments of their services with the amendments tabled by the coalition groups were largely taken into account. With regard to the amendments of the SDS Group, she pointed out that if they were to be adopted, certain nomotechnical corrections, such as the renumbering of paragraphs and the correction of references in the law, would be necessary. The opinion of the Commission of the State Regulation was presented by the President of their Commission, who said that the Commission had raised some questions the applicant had asked for clarifications, namely the proposed provision whereby the manufacturer of pyrotechnic articles could produce and use pyrotechnic articles for his own use, without having to carry out the conformity procedure and obtain the CE markings; however, he must obtain the authorisation of the Ministry of Interior and the extension of the powers of the Inspectorate of Internal Affairs. The Commission commended the activities of the applicants in the process of harmonising the bill as it was in line with the professional public and the local communities; it supported the proposal. In the debate on individual members, the members and members of the Committee welcomed the introduction of greater responsibilities of economic operators throughout the supply chain with the aim of protecting public interest. With regard to the extension of the powers and actions of the inspectors, the question of the capacity of the inspectorate and his willingness to carry out the new tasks was raised. Cases of use of pyrotechnic articles showing their danger and threat to humans, animals and the environment have also been highlighted. Therefore, the view was expressed that it was necessary to consider the complete prohibition of fireworks, namely category 1, 2 and 3; whose main effect is pok. The SDS Group has tabled an amendment with the above content. In view of the applicant's explanations that the prohibition on the sale and use of category 1 fireworks, the main effect of which is pok, was contrary to the Directive, it was proposed to consider an appropriate adjustment of the age limit or an increase in the age limit of 14 and 16 years laid down in the applicable law concerning the sale and use of pyrotechnic articles. The representative of the applicant was asked for a clear answer, in which category the firecrackers belong and whether their sale and use are allowed in us. The Committee adopted amendments to the SMC, Desus and SD groups to Articles 2, 3, 6, 8, 10, 16, 17, 19, and 22 to 29. The Committee did not accept the amendment of the SDS Group to Article 15 and the amendment of the SDS Group to Article 25 became irrelevant. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the complementary draft law, which includes the amendments adopted, is an integral part of this report.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u40	Thank you. The position of the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats will be presented by Mr Jernej Vrtovec, please.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u41	Dear colleagues and colleagues, Mr President! The purpose of the Explosive and Pyrotechnic Products Act is to regulate the production, transport and use of these products with a view to ensuring the safety of professional users and consumers of explosives and pyrotechnic articles. This area is also subject to the regulation of European legislation. The two Directives adopted by the European Union have already been implemented into the law in force. However, two other Directives have been adopted, namely 2013 and 2014, which are the subject of the amendments currently under consideration and the addition of this Act. Both Directives increase the liability of economic operators, producers and all entities in the supply or sale chain of explosives and pyrotechnics. The responsibility of the manufacturer of the explosives themselves is increased. The manufacturer is the one who knows the most about the design and production process of the products, so he must ensure that they are manufactured in accordance with safety requirements. It is obliged to draw up appropriate documentation and to ensure that the product complies with European Union legislation. The product therefore bears the CE marking in such a case. A national contact point is established for explosives and pyrotechnics, through which all international communications are carried out, namely this point of the MNZ. The bill also provides for broader powers and possibilities to act on the inspection of explosives and pyrotechnic means. Thus, the inspector has the duty to re-check the conformity of explosives or pyrotechnic articles available only on our internal market if he considers that it poses a risk to the life, health and safety of humans, property and the environment. If, upon re-checking the product, it is found that the product does not meet the safety requirements, the inspector may order the legal person to remedy the deficiencies and set the appropriate time limit for that purpose. In the event that the deficiencies are therefore not remedied within a specified period, or if it is found that they cannot be remedied, the inspector may impose restrictions or prohibitions on the availability of explosives or pyrotechnic articles. The inspection shall also obtain broader powers in the control of products available in other Member States of the European Union. If it is found that the product does not meet the safety requirements, it is obliged to take all necessary measures to ensure the safety of people and property in our country, while at the same time informing the European Commission and other EU Member States of the measures taken. Members of the New Slovenia Group – Christian Democrats will support the proposed amendments and additions to the Explosive and Pyrotechnic Resources Act, as it brings solutions that ensure a higher level of health, safety and public safety, while also ensuring fair competition on the European Union market. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u42	Thank you. The position of the Parliamentary Group of the Mir Cerar Party will be presented by the mag. Dušan Verbich.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u43	Thank you for your word, Vice President. Dear Members and Members of Parliament, a lovely greeting! With the proposal for a law that we are considering today, two Directives of the European Parliament and of the Council are transposed into the Slovenian legal order, as has already been said, on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the availability of pyrotechnic articles on the market, on renewal and on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States concerning the making available of explosives for civil use on the market and on their control. An essential amendment to the law is to increase the responsibility of economic operators to comply with explosives and pyrotechnic articles in order to ensure a high level of protection of public interest. The proposed amendment introduces the obligations of manufacturers to plan and produce products in accordance with the highest safety requirements in order to produce technical documentation and to attach the product to the instructions for use and safety information in the Slovenian language. The law treats importers and distributors in the same way as the manufacturer and imposes the same obligations on them as regards the adoption of appropriate withdrawal, recall and sales ban measures. The law introduces new obligations and powers to the Inspectors of the Republic of Slovenia for internal affairs regarding procedures for handling explosive and pyrotechnic articles which pose a risk to the life, health and safety of humans. The next thing that we think is very important is that this amendment of the law also refers to the so-called labelling of explosives. As far as pyrotechnics are concerned, the traceability system. I think it is right to say this out loud because of the importance, because the purpose of such labelling is primarily to prevent abuse and place, which is a great help to law enforcement authorities in following up on abuses, losses and thefts of explosive and pyrotechnic products. What is important to point out is the so-called security aspect which must be ensured by this law – at least by the State, that products presenting certain risks are safely used. We received a letter from the SMC Group prior to the consideration of this law concerning the use of firecrackers and other fireworks, which are very disturbing for most citizens as well as pets. The Committee has also been exposed to what injury may be caused by the reckless use of these funds; but we must stress that the sale and use of firecrackers is already prohibited, and the purchase of these is taking place on the illegal market. But we are very happy and welcome the decision that in the framework of the Board of Uniform Police, the Ministry of Education and Sport and the Institute for Health Protection was once again carried out in December 2014 a preventive action by Be a star, not a firecracker! And as they say in the analysis, the habits of users of pyrotechnic means change to the desired direction, to the welfare of all people. We will support the bill in the SMC Group. ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u49	Dear Vice President, colleagues and colleagues! Allow me to announce in the introduction the support of the Social Democrats for the proposed amendment to the Explosives and Pyrotechnic Products Act. Not only because we introduce into the Slovenian legal order two revised directives concerning the accessibility of pyrotechnic articles on the market and the availability of explosives for civil use on the market and their control; but also because increased liability of economic operators is introduced; from manufacturers, importers and distributors of explosive or pyrotechnic articles. The economic operators in this chain therefore undertake to provide safety and other regulatory requirements for the manufacture of explosive and pyrotechnic articles. In particular, in the event of non-compliance, they themselves must take appropriate measures, including recalling or withdrawing explosive or pyrotechnic articles from the market. The proposed Act amending and supplementing the Explosives and Pyrotechnic Products Act also introduces some important amendments and additions relating to the introduction of additional powers and measures by explosive inspectors or pyrotechnic articles which pose a risk at national or European Union level – ensuring traceability of explosive devices with a registered identification number; traceability of pyrotechnic articles with a registration number and the possibility of withdrawing the already issued notification certificate for explosives or pyrotechnic articles placed on the market if they are found to be inconsistent with the essential safety requirements which could not be eliminated by re-evaluation. It is not to be overlooked that important objectives have already been achieved with the new law in 2008. The sale, possession and use of category 2 and 3 fireworks, the main effect of which is pok, was prohibited. There are firecrackers in the category that we all know more or less. The sale of category 1 fireworks, the main effect of which is pok, is only allowed from 19 to 21 December and can only be used from 26 December to 2 January. Here we count dog bombs, remorseful confetti and several other products. These products pose a very small risk and cause a negligible level of noise. The pyrotechnic articles must not be used in cramped dwellings, in buildings, in all closed spaces, in the vicinity of hospitals, in passenger transport vehicles, in public meetings and in public events. Legally, the use of sales has already been largely done, but we believe that no such restrictive ban on the use of pyrotechnic means will eradicate this. Let's ask ourselves why? But above all, who enjoys buying firecrackers and rockets, do they use them? We don't think they're our kids. It is the responsibility of our parents to teach our children and adolescents that the use of these products is very dangerous; that the reckless, reckless and frivolous use of pyrotechnic products causes too many serious injuries, such as burns of the face, eye injuries, hearing injuries, ruptures – usually of the hands. Youths need to be explained that all these injuries have affected us for the rest of our lives. Data on the number of injured persons, violations in the use of pyrotechnic articles and other relevant data in this area are collected in police website reports. However, I will allow myself to have a more recent analysis of the problem of the use of pyrotechnic products during Christmas and New Year's holidays 2013-2014, and I quote: "In 2013 we continued with the action Bodi Star, do not throw a firecracker! The user's habits in the use of pyrotechnic articles have proven to be changing in the desired direction. We can very well assess the performance of the tasks and activities of police administrations and police stations, which have led or engaged in various projects at regional and local level. However, the problematic use remains contrary to the manufacturer’s instructions, its own manufacture or processing, the use of pyrotechnic articles in other objects and the use of products purchased on the black market." The measures and preventive actions carried out by the public authorities and the State in the long term will produce positive results. Social Democrats, however, believe that we can make a big contribution to this by giving up the use of pyrotechnics. But if not without them, we use them with extreme care and care that we do not harm ourselves and others. We use where we do not disturb people, especially children, the elderly, and the sick. ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u51	Dear President, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! Allow me to briefly define myself to the amendments and novels of the Explosives and Pyrotechnics Act. As you note, the main objective of the proposed law is to transpose the provisions of EU directives into the legal order of the Republic of Slovenia. Directives 2013 and 2014 on the harmonisation of the laws of the Member States relating to the making available and supervision of explosives of one Directive and pyrotechnic articles of another Directive. Both Directives and the proposal for a law are based on the introduction of increased liability for all economic operators in the entire supply chain — manufacturers, importers and distributors of pyrotechnic articles and explosives; by ensuring that explosives and pyrotechnic articles placed on the market comply with essential safety requirements and that, in the event of their non-compliance, all necessary measures are taken if their withdrawal or recall is also required. The draft law also lays down additional powers and measures of inspectors relating to explosives and pyrotechnic articles which constitute a risk either at national level or at European Union level. And I can say that the Internal Affairs Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia will also be strengthened to this end, including through internal redeployments. It therefore gives the Ministry of the Interior the opportunity to withdraw the certificates of notification already issued for explosives and pyrotechnic articles placed on the market, provided that the familiar evaluation reveals that their compliance with the essential safety requirements could not be eliminated at that time. In order to ensure traceability, as you can see, it is also stated that all the explosives put on the market by manufacturers must be fitted with a unique identification number, while pyrotechnic articles must be marked with a registration number. It is also the duty of manufacturers and wholesalers to keep records of the type and quantity of explosives produced, sold and destroyed by electronic means; and of all explosive users to keep the records electronically or in the form of books. They are all required to keep those records. However, they must also allow the supervisory authorities to check the records beyond normal regular working hours. The applicant therefore considers the solutions presented to be appropriate and proposes that the draft law be adopted at a meeting of the National Assembly. At the same time, we propose that, in accordance with the Rules of Procedure, the law be dealt with at the same meeting. With regard to the amendment tabled by the SDS Group to Article 26 of the draft law, by which the SDS Group proposes that the Internal Affairs Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia, under inspection by pyrotechnics traders, pay particular attention to the implementation of Article 35 of the Act. The findings and proposals to improve the situation in the field of the sale of fireworks and security would also form an integral part of the annual report of the Ministry of the Interior. The Government does not support this amendment, since the Act on explosives and pyrotechnic articles already in force in Article 36 provides that the Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Home Affairs shall carry out inspections on the implementation of this Act and on the basis of its rules issued. This means that the Inspectorate is already in charge of inspection of section 35 of the Act. Therefore, additional, special attention is not required. Inspectors must also respect the public principle in their work. The latter is defined in Article 6 of the Inspection Control Act, which also provides that inspectors shall, on the basis of and within the limits of the powers of the chairman, inform the public of their findings and measures, if necessary; to protect the rights of legal and natural persons; and, where necessary, to ensure respect for the legal order or their provisions. In addition, I can say that the Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia for Home Affairs also reports on the operation and inspections carried out each year, highlighting also the essential findings of inspections in all areas of its work, including in the field of control of pyrotechnic products. All reports are accessible to everyone and are also published on the Inspectorate website where they can be viewed. In addition to the Internal Affairs Inspectorate of the Republic of Slovenia, the police also exercise control over the provision of Article 35 of the Explosives and Pyrotechnic Products Act. For preventive purposes, the police have been involved, as you know, in the action of Bodi Star, in cooperation with the Ministry of Education and the Institute of Health! It also analyses the use of pyrotechnic articles and also publishes them online. So I can say, as my fellow Member has said, that in one of the house searches, a minor found 7,000 pieces of pyrotechnic articles to be able to control it works well. The definition of the amendment of the coalition groups to Article 28 of the draft law, and this proposal follows the opinion of the Legislative and Legal Service of the National Assembly, i.e. it is a nomotechnic amendment to the reference to a substantive provision and the latter amendment is also supported by the Government. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u53	Thank you very much, Vice President. Nice greetings, Members and Members of Parliament! My debate will focus mainly on greater and better awareness of the so-called obligations relating to explosive and pyrotechnic products. In view of the many things that have already been said, I do not think there should be too many warnings about the dangers arising from these so-called explosive and pyrotechnic objects. As has been mentioned many times in the introductory presentations, the main reason for amending this law is the two Directives. Here I should simply touch once again the content itself, as far as these directives are concerned, and what this means for our amendment to the law. In order to protect consumers, the Directives put a number of safety requirements at the forefront. As already mentioned, manufacturers must carry out a series of tests for explosive and pyrotechnic products before they are placed on the market to demonstrate their chemical and physical, mineral and safety characteristics. At the same time, manufacturers must also demonstrate that they have a permanent quality assurance system in place in production or that they are capable of ensuring the continued quality of their products. Each new product must be sent for testing to an independent laboratory which must comply with the requirements of the Directive and also of the Council. The competent authority of the country in which the laboratory is located shall, in cooperation with other national authorities, periodically verify the quality assurance system in the production itself by one of the modules set out in the decision of the European Commission. This is the conformity assessment procedure by which the manufacturer receives the so-called conformity certificate for his product; and only he can mark it with the so-called CE marking. This means that the CE marking is a product of constant quality and meets all the technical requirements which are the same for the entire European Union. Any explosive and pyrotechnic article which has this certificate of conformity may appear anywhere on the European market and States may not, by their legislation, impose any additional conditions to impede their market intervention. I believe that this is important, although it is almost universally known that we are once again aware of this. I would touch on another area, which was also exposed to the parental committee; only on the grave. Classification of pyrotechnic articles by category. According to our regulations, we have three groups. In the first group, fireworks are classified in four categories: first, second, third and fourth. I would point out only the first, which has also been mentioned most frequently in the debate. And the first category is a fireworks product, which poses a very small risk, causes negligible noise levels and is intended for use in compressed settlements, including fireworks, which are intended for use in residential buildings and other closed spaces. The second group of pyrotechnic products is simply mentioned as being only stage scenes. Here are two groups and in the third category, the remaining pyrotechnic products, which certainly also pose a certain danger, mean a great deal of care by their handling. What is important for all of us users, especially parents, is set out in Article 35 – Restrictions on the sale and use of pyrotechnic articles. The pyrotechnic articles of each category are not allowed to be sold to natural persons who are younger; 14 years for category 1; 16 years for category 2; 18 years for category P1 and T1. What does that mean? Notwithstanding the foregoing, the use of category 1 fireworks is also allowed for natural persons under the age of 14 and 16 if I add category 2; however, what is important if they are under the supervision of parents or guardians. On this occasion, I'd like to mention something else. The seller may require a natural person who considers that he does not meet the age conditions just mentioned to require him or her to demonstrate his or her age in advance by means of a public document proving the identity of the person. I would like to re-emphasize another category concerning the sale, possession and use of category 2 and category 3 fireworks products known as firecrackers. It should be reiterated that this is prohibited in the Republic of Slovenia. The sale of category 1 fireworks is returned to them, the main effect of which is pok, is allowed from 19 to 31 December and their use is allowed only from 26 December to 2 January. I conclude this part with the following definition, for which it is right to get to know each other again. The following are prohibited for natural persons: processing of pyrotechnic articles to increase the effect, use of pyrotechnic articles for other purposes, own manufacture of pyrotechnic articles or mixtures, and resale of pyrotechnic articles. Firecrackers from the black market are the greatest threat, either because they are of poor quality or even have serial errors. I am confident that, with the amendment of this law and all the activities to be carried out by the Ministry of Internal Affairs officials, police officers and inspectors will have much work to do in the coming time to detect all violators of the law for the safety of users and the accidental passovers found during the pyrotechnic fire. Let us hope that they can achieve consistent compliance with the law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u65	Madam Vice President, thank you for your input. Good-bye, everybody! The United Left Group has tabled an amendment to the Law on Marriage and Family Relations with one single and simple reason to eliminate the unconstitutional situation and systematic discrimination against same-sex partners. Discrimination afforded by the current legal order of the Republic of Slovenia. This proposal therefore ensures equality before the law. Article 14 of the Constitution of the Republic of Slovenia clearly states that "in Slovenia everyone is guaranteed equal human rights and fundamental freedoms regardless of nationality, race, sex, language, religion, political or other belief, material status, birth, education, social situation, disability or any other personal situation". Article 14 of the Constitution therefore prohibits discrimination in the provision, exercise or protection of human rights and fundamental freedoms in the light of individual personal circumstances, which undoubtedly include sexual orientation. We can therefore talk about discrimination when the state treats individuals differently in the same situations. In the case of same-sex and diversified partners, as the Constitutional Court also points out, in essence, the same factual and legal basis for living communities. Any distinction in the legal regime of both communities, including the legal consequences of these communities, is not based on some real non-personal circumstances, but on sexual orientation. In cases of same-sex and diversified partners, the key feature of the relationship is the stable relationship of two people close to each other, helping each other and supporting each other. In view of the fact that this is the same situation, any distinction should be regarded as discrimination in the light of personal circumstances. The current situation is therefore contrary to the Constitution and places same-sex couples in an unequal position and discriminates against them. It is only from the fact that one group of individuals is married to another group of individuals, another group of individuals, that there is no equal treatment. The proposal for a law tabled by the United Left Group has the same objective and principle: to abolish provisions which make the law and non-legislatively anti-constitutional and discriminatory as a community of two persons of the opposite sex, and thus understand gender and sexual orientation as the personal circumstance on which individual rights are derived. The bill thus introduces a new concept of marriage and extramarital union, which may or may exist regardless of gender between the two partners. Based on the findings of the Constitutional Court that two persons of the same sex are considered to be the same legal and factual basis of the community as the marriage of two persons of different sexes, and relating to the requirement for a legal state and to the full equality of the amendment, the marriage community is defined as a living community of two persons. Consequently, the proposal recognises the legal consequences of the life of two persons of the same sex who have not entered into a marriage, but there are no reasons why the marriage between them would be invalid. In the Group, the United Left considers that this proposal, which is, inter alia, consistent with the professional public and the competent ministries and is fully supported by the unified LGBT community, is the only systematic solution to the anti-constitutional and discriminatory situation in the Republic of Slovenia. Dear Members, dear Members, we must be aware that being free is not just a right, it requires more. To live in a way that respects and promotes the freedom of others. If you are married or have the same rights as married couples in an extramarital community, ask yourself why you would have this right and deny it to others. I am pleased that today the rights that we and my wife have will also be recognized by many same-sex couples, who today are unjustifiably denied. This will move forward towards a truly tolerant and inclusive society. With this step Slovenia is now entering the 21st century. Thank you.

This is also not based on the reasoning of the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia, on the contrary, the Constitutional Court has taken the view that the same partnership is a relationship which, by its content, is merely a similar marriage. So it's about communities that are separate by nature. The Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia has never repealed the entire law on the registration of same-sex communities, which would have done so if it considered that the law violated article 14 of the Constitution of Slovenia on the prohibition of discrimination. This means that the law proposes that two persons of the same sex adopt a child or are entitled to artificial insemination, even though both persons are fertile. This would allow the State, systematically and not objectively, to raise children without a father or without a mother, thus pursuing exactly the opposite objective, as set out in Article 53 of the Constitution of Slovenia, in order to protect the State's family, motherhood and fatherhood and create the necessary conditions for this protection. The General Declaration on Human Rights and Freedoms of the UN also imposes a duty to protect the family, which it identifies as a fundamental cell, and it is the responsibility of both the State and society to protect. The proposal for this law abolishes the sole definition of husband and wife. Since the adoption of this marriage, husbands will no longer be husbands, and wives will no longer be wives, but they will be two persons. As a result, husbands and wives will no longer be subject to other legal provisions, including the assumption that the husband is the father of a child born in a marriage. The law will allow the adoption of a child to two persons of the same sex, but it will not allow the adoption of a child to old parents in case the child loses the parents. We think it's a marriage that's very poorly prepared. It shall be adopted by means of a reduced procedure based on the abuse of rules of procedure. There was no public debate before we got him and the Members at the table. In the SDS, we support the rights of same-sex couples when it comes to social and natural rights between adults. Adoption of a child is not an adult's human right; adults cannot ask the country to do so. It is the child who is the bearer of the human right to be adopted into the community of men and women, that is, into an environment that is as close as possible to the environment in which he lived when he lost his parents. The task of society is to support the child's primary need to live with his mother and father. In our country, it seems that we have just the problem of taking care of families to enable children to live a decent life with their mother and father. For example, we do not say that two men cannot be a good father, but we say that none of them can be a good mother. The adoption of this law will be a complete ignorance of the democratic consensus. Therefore, and because of the proposal in the SDS for a law amending and supplementing the Law on Marriage and Family Relations, we will not support it. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u88	Thank you very much. Ms Violeta Tomić will present the position of the Joint Left Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u89	Dear Vice President, dear colleagues! In the Group of Members, the United Left believes that after more than 20 years of fighting to equalize the rights of same-sex and diversified partners, 18 years after making a similar 1997 proposal and many public debates and media campaigns that have already drawn up all the views and arguments, now is finally the time for us to take on responsibility without interference and to ensure equality before the law for all citizens and residents of Slovenia. We have thus tabled the novel ZZDR for a simple reason, by redefining the legal community into a simple but systematic and comprehensive way to eliminate the anti-constitutional situation and discrimination of same-sex partners, which is made possible by the current legal order of the Republic of Slovenia. The proposal on the table today will thus give couples the rights and obligations that other couples already have. In this context, it is simply about ensuring equality in accordance with the Slovenian Constitution, in particular Article 14 thereof, which stipulates that we are all equal before the law and that any distinction on the basis of personal circumstances is unconstitutional and discriminatory. In the current legal order of the Republic of Slovenia, it is clear from the way it is regulated, that we have one group of individuals and one group of individuals of another, that it is not equal treatment. However, it is crucial that the same-sex partners in Slovenia are violated certain fundamental social, economic and legal rights, such as the right to leave work due to illness of the partner or partner, the right to compensation for income for the care of the family member, the right to health insurance by partner, the right to claim material compensation for mental pain in the face of death or severe disability of the partner and many others. As he says, such discrimination and the adoption of legislation on the table today are also bound by the two judgments of the Constitutional Court of the Republic of Slovenia in 2009 and 2013, which establish that they are essentially the same and diversified partnership. This means that there is no just and factual or legal reason for unequal treatment before the law. Even in the wider European context, the legal trend in this area is clear. Every month, we are witnessing new judgments by constitutional and international courts which recognise the same rights for same-sex couples in many areas of life. The trend is quite clear and is heading towards a perfect equation, which is only a matter of time. Already 25 years ago, Denmark, as the first country in Europe, adopted legislation on a registered partnership. 15 years ago, the Netherlands completely aligned the rights of same-sex and diversified couples, and today 11 European countries already know the legislation we are proposing. And it was not in any of these countries that the black scenarios feared by the opponents of the new ZDR were realized. In Slovenia, we have been discussing the equalisation of rights since 1989, but so far there have been no systematic solutions. If Slovenia was the first of neighbouring countries to regulate this area 10 years ago, though poorly, most of us are now ahead of schedule. Hungary, Austria and even Croatia have more advanced legislation than Slovenia. Today we have the opportunity to re-establish ourselves as a progressive, democratic and tolerant society in this area and join countries that have already fully established equality between diversified and same-sex couples. So today is the opportunity for Slovenia to enter the 21st century. In the United Left Group, we therefore believe that our proposal, which is consistent with the professional public and the competent ministries and is fully supported by the unified LGBT community, is the only and necessary systematic solution to the anti-constitutional and discriminatory situation in the Republic of Slovenia. The proposal does not introduce anything radical but simply follows the fundamental principles of democratic society, the results of more than a decade of civil dialogue and public debate. In our efforts to democratise all spheres of social life, socialists are also stressing that now is not the time for new debates, but the actual changes brought by the proposed law. Changes with which no one loses anything, but many will gain much. Thank you.

Most of the studies analysed show that families with two dedicated and loving parents, regardless of their sex, offer a favourable environment for their development, including one or more parental families. It is true that the social environment in which children grow up in families with same-sex partners is not entirely identical to the social environment in which children grow up in families with partners of different sexes, but scientific knowledge shows that their development in no key respect is different from the development of children growing up in families of different sexes. Studies also show that children of different and same sexes in terms of developmental deficits, such as anxiety, depression, self-image, social adaptability and similar aspects, do not differ. When viewed strictly methodologically, according to some criteria, investing in parenthood and parental skills, two women on average play the same good parenting role as men and women in the traditional division of parental roles. These taxes also include compensation for parental care. This means that all homosexuals pay contributions while they have no right to use it. Nor can a homosexual couple benefit from the right to maternity leave and paternity leave, each of the partners doing something because certain rights are not transferable. But the law provides for a mother and a father, not two partners. So homosexuals have obligations, but not rights. As we have already heard, the 2005 law governs registered same-sex partnerships. It provides partners with a limited number of rights and obligations which mainly relate to their relationships: the right to subsistence and subsistence, the right to acquire common assets, the right to housing, the right to inheritance and the right to obtain information on the health status of the affected partner and visits to health homes. ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u109	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Good-bye, everybody! This morning, when I arrived at work, I was in a mailbox similar to you, probably waiting for some 486 mails that urged us to save the family, save the Slovenian nation from the fall and warn that the old Greek and Roman empires had collapsed because of the law we are adopting today. This has also somehow encouraged me to think, like the ministers before, that in the public debate about what is actually being adopted today, there has been a major misunderstanding or disagreement, if such panic reactions are possible, and that is why I will use the first introductory speech to explain once again exactly what exactly is what we are accepting today. Today, we do not change anything other than Article 3 of the Law on Marriage and Family Relations, in which the formulation: "marital union is a community of men and women" is replaced by the formulation "marital union is a community of two people". So the word husband and wife is replaced by the words of two people. That's all. We're not taking anything, we're just thinking about justice for those who don't have it yet. In this way, the problem of discrimination between same-sex couples, which has dragged on in Slovenia for decades, is being addressed in an elegant and simple way. With this simple change, same-sex couples finally acquire the economic, legal and social rights that belong to marriage, including the right to health insurance by partner, the right to compensation for income for the care of a family member, the right to absence from work if the partner is sick, and, of course, the opportunity to run for adoption of a child. In addition, this change eliminates the unconstitutional situation in Slovenia. Article 14 of our Constitution provides that everyone is guaranteed the same human rights regardless of his nationality, race, sex, language, religion or any other personal circumstance. One of these circumstances is sexual orientation. And so far, the regulation in Slovenia has discriminated more than clearly against people because of sexual orientation. By today’s adoption of the amendments to the law that was written in 1976, as we have heard before, we are finally removing this unconstitutional situation and we are bringing Slovenia closer to the standards that are in force in the 21st century. Similar solutions are in force in 11 European countries and 37 United States states. And the effects are very positive everywhere. Nowhere has the end of the world happened, from which some warn us. In this light, I am truly surprised by the moral panic caused by some religious communities and political initiatives. Yesterday, in the radio show Studio will listen to the disastrous scenarios that this law would lead to the firing of Christians from the public sector, the establishment of ethical groups of commissions that would pursue otherwise thinking and somehow get the feeling that they want to convince us that we are pushing the way of some kind of Orwell totalitarianism that we could read in his book 1984. On behalf of the proposer, i.e. the United Left, I would like to reiterate this point and ensure that in the proposed amendment of the law it is nothing more than that the formulation of "the marriage is a community of men and women" is replaced by the formulation "the marriage is a union of two persons". It is easy to eliminate discrimination in this area, and this is also the only purpose of the novel. I am pleased that today, in Parliament, a large majority is made up of both the coalition and the opposition, which is aware of these injustices and is prepared to be eliminated. The right-wing third of Parliament, which still defends and discriminates against existing legislation, I call on it not to be subject to moral panic, but rather to follow the example of the conservative prime minister of Britain, David Cameron, who said: "I support the equality of same-sex couples, not even though I am conservative, but precisely because I am conservative." Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u114	Thank you very much. Dr. Jasna Murgel has the word. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u115	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. Dear Minister, colleagues and colleagues! I would also very much like to thank all those who sent me e-mails, there were over 100 e-mails calling on me to vote against this law. I respect the voter opinion, they can have it, but I also have an opinion, and I can't accept it because it's different from theirs. Now I'll explain why. We talk a lot about the concept of discrimination, and it might be good to say what discrimination is. This was taught by my son in the fifth grade of elementary school, about which I taught students of international law in college; so we can help ourselves with the definition of one of the international instruments relating to discrimination by definition of the Convention on the Prohibition of All Forms of Racial Discrimination, but the definition is the same, the circumstances are only different. Thus, the expression discrimination under this Convention, of which Slovenia is also a Party, means "any distinction, exclusion, restriction or prioritisation on the basis of any personal circumstance which is intended or has a real effect in preventing or denying anyone for the equal recognition, enjoyment or exercise of human rights and fundamental freedoms in the political, economic, social, cultural and every other area of public life." So that's discrimination. The prohibition of discrimination is one of the fundamental norms of international law that has been established in the long process following World War II. Why? Above all, because in World War II human rights were massively violated, we know what was happening when one common thought was more valuable than the other, and what was happening in World War II. So, after World War II, a body of international human rights law was formed. And under this international law, the scope of the specific circumstances under which discrimination is prohibited is increasing. The prohibition of discrimination is thus enshrined in international instruments, we have listed them earlier, United Nations documents, the General Declaration on Human Rights, the International Covenant on Political and Civil Rights, on Economic and Social Rights, the European Convention on Human Rights. The prohibition of discrimination, of course, was passed on to our constitution, in 14. The article we are talking about, which is the first article in the chapter of our Constitution, which opens the chapter on fundamental human rights and fundamental freedoms. On the basis of the Slovenian Constitution, we have also said this many times today, discrimination is prohibited on the basis of any personal circumstances. And one of these circumstances is sexual orientation. It has been found, in many studies, that the existing legal system does not provide equal opportunities for same-sex partners in the Slovenian legal order. Yes, there is a law on the registration of the same-sex partnership community, but it has not completely equalized the possibility of same-sex communities, which is so much more applicable to unregistered partnerships. All that is to be achieved by this amendment of the law is that these options are equal. The very definition of this law on the registration of the same-sex legal community is, in my opinion, discrimination, because, as has already been mentioned, and I hope that it will be mentioned again, you cannot register the partnership community as if you were registering a car. This registration itself means different treatment and discrimination. We have also mentioned several times that, therefore, the real equality of the same-sex community in Slovenia is not that there are many laws in which partners of same-sex communities cannot exercise their rights in the same way as husband and wife in the same-sex community as defined in the Marriage and Family Relations Act. For example, the Bond Code — the right to compensation in the event of death or the severe disability of a family member is limited to spouses, extramarital partners, children or parents of the deceased, and not to the same - sex partner. The Housing Code – the definition of narrower family members for the purposes of this law only mentions the marriage and extramarital partner, not the registered or unregistered sex partner. The Health Care Act – the employee and the unregistered same-sex partner does not have the right to leave work due to the illness of the partner and the right to compensation for income for the care of the family member. There are a lot of these laws, a lot of laws that talk mainly about a marriage partner, an extramarital partner, possibly about a registered one, or all of these concepts are mixed and therefore partners in the same-sex partnerships are in an unequally basic position and are therefore discriminated against. Let me repeat what I said on the committee, because it repeats itself repeatedly, yes, and now most are discriminated against. Most cannot be discriminated against by nature. The meaning of the prohibition of discrimination is precisely to ensure the protection of the rights of a group of people who are in a minority and who are, because of some personal situation, such as disability, age, gender, anything, sexual orientation, can be discriminated against because they do not have that tool, there is no majority who decide what rights some communities will be recognised. Most of them have the power to take decisions that are in their favour through Parliament through the authorities representing the decision-making authorities. It is therefore necessary to protect the rights of persons in a deprived situation, not the protection of the majority. Let us once again understand this concept of what protection of rights means. And the protection of human rights is the protection of rights in relation to the State, individuals and groups in relation to the State. Dear colleagues and colleagues! I think that the issue that we are discussing today, which we are going to decide about, is very important, because it means a shift in some thought and at the very beginning of the regulation of protection against discrimination. Today, and as before, on the Committee on Labour, Social and Family Affairs, we will decide whether we will indeed be a democratic country and we will also ensure equal opportunities for partner same-sex communities or will still have to register and still have fewer rights than the partners in the marriage community, as now defined in the law in force. If we choose the first option, we will join the advanced democratic states. If we choose this option, it is also important that I highlight something. If we choose the first option, so we vote in favour of the law and against the amendments, we choose, in fact, to fulfil the obligation, the legal obligation of the State of the Republic of Slovenia, which is a party to international instruments, to international treaties setting out obligations for the protection of human rights. I have already mentioned the International Covenant on Civil and Political Rights and the same Article is in the International Covenant on Economic and Social Rights, which provides in the second paragraph of Article 2 that each State Party to the Covenant is obliged to take urgent measures in accordance with its constitutional procedures and the provisions of this Covenant in order to adopt such legal or other measures as may be necessary to exercise the rights recognised in this Covenant. This pact provides for a prohibition of discrimination. Our obligation under international law, not only through our Constitution, and international law is part of our internal law, read Article 8 of the Constitution, we have an obligation to ensure proper implementation of the prohibition of discrimination. Under the third paragraph of that Article, the Parties must ensure that, inter alia, the competent authorities and any other bodies decide on the rights of the person lodging the complaint and must also develop the possibilities for judicial protection. In short, to the fullest extent at all social levels, the State is obliged to provide protection against discrimination to those who may be discriminated against with equal baselines. And there's nothing else we can guarantee. We don't want to support if we support this law. I agree with the bill's proponents that if we adopt this law, we will enter into the 21st.
