Students learn that people in the community are different, for example, by age, religion, sexual orientation, ethnicity, socioeconomic status. Furthermore, they learn social, cultural differences and recognize stereotypes in connection with them. And also, they learn different forms of association between people in the community. So the school curriculum is already at various stages for consideration of differences between people, where the learning goal is tolerance and respect for diversity. Special attention in the curriculum is paid to didactic recommendations, i.e. the choice of methods and forms of teaching, the development of different socio-cognitive and social skills. Parents also have ensured cooperation with the school by including themselves in various school bodies such as the school world, the parent world, and in this way they can influence the educational work of the school. The new law fully defines certain learning objectives and competences. I agree with the dean of the Faculty of Education in Ljubljana that the issue of family and family relations in schools, regardless of the law or novelty, must be dealt with in a critical manner; that is, by respecting the principles of objectivity and criticism. The school must convey knowledge and enable the student to be critical. A little earlier, a colleague mentioned or wondered how teachers should be objective in dealing with such sensitive topics and issues if they have a different belief. Please, teachers are doing their work professionally and ethically, this is their mission and this comment may also be an insult to teachers. Dear colleagues, finally, Slovenia is a party to the European Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms and to the concept of human rights and duties as a fundamental ethical and legal norm. Thank you.

We are not introducing innovation, but we are simply regulating the legal status and hence the resulting social and economic rights of already existing communities. We do not destroy society or family, we simply redefine the marriage community and thus make family life possible for same - sex couples. We do not destroy the nation, we guarantee the same rights and equal treatment before the law only to all citizens and residents of Slovenia. We do not accept the novels past the public debate and quickly, as some of you have told us, but it is a proposal that is in line with the professional public and the competent ministries and is fully supported by the unified LGTB community. This is therefore a proposal resulting from more than a decade of civil dialogue and public debate. All we do is comprehensively and systematically solve the problem of discrimination and unequal treatment of same-sex partners. When the marriage is passed, no one loses anything, we'll leave all the mothers, all the fathers will remain fathers, all the spouses will remain husbands and wives, and many will gain enormously. Believe me, we're not taking anything from anyone, and many of you, too, are getting huge in your vicinity. Thank you.

Finally, it is right to look at the content of this proposed amendment to the law on marriage and family relations in order to remember what is really changing. The change is that if this law is enforced, two persons of the same sex will be able to marry in addition to two persons of different sexes. The same applies to the extramarital community. Thus, the unaffiliated community will be considered to be a community of both men and women and a community of two persons of the same sex. However, the law further regulates the transitional period when it enters into force and what happens to those registered partner communities that already exist and are registered under the so-called ZRIPS, namely the Law on the Registration of the same-sex partnership, so that two persons who have this registration can declare that they want to be transformed into a marriage or that they do not want to be transformed, then this does not change so that they can also decide on any changes in their surnames. This is the content of this novel. We have, of course, gone a long way in the debate, but it is only right that we, at the conclusion of the debate, really calmly remember what this is all about. The Law on the Registration of the Same-Sex Partner Community set out exactly what the name of the law already says. Registered. Two persons of the same sex registered on the administrative unit and, after they had this registration concluded, they were subject to a limited fee-based set of rights they had. Why do we consider that the parliamentary inquiry is of public interest or public interest and that it must be carried out by the National Assembly? Very briefly. In 2012, a proposal for a guarantee law for block TES 6 was tabled in the National Assembly, provided the investment price of the project is reduced and the price of lignite is reduced. Alstom, however, began threatening to stop works on the project due to delays in his guarantee. In June, the National Assembly then adopted the State Guarantee Act for a loan of EUR 440 million, and among the conditions for the conclusion of the guarantee were the condition that the project should not be extorted from the existing EUR 303 million and that the price of lignite should not exceed EUR 2.25 per gigajoule. Soon thereafter, the price at present, as we said, increased by a billion 400 and a few million more, the price of lignite increased, requirements for additional preheating boilers were put in place. Last year, when the coalition was already falling apart, the National Assembly adopted a tax double, namely the amendment of the Law on Taxation on the Income of Legal Persons and the amendment of the Income Act and raised the threshold for so-called flatlists, of course, it is a small economy, with 50,000 annual turnover per 100,000 annual traffic. These two novels entered into force with 1. 1. 2015, but the effects of these two laws are somehow paralysed. Why? This is because those who are liable to value added tax and who are obliged to enter the VAT system at 50,000 turnover do not actually benefit from the removal of administrative barriers. We have already written twice to the government, formerly and also to current Dr. Mir Cerar, and have proposed to amend article 94 of the Value Added Tax Act and to raise the threshold for compulsory entry into the VAT system from 50,000 to 100,000. This would somehow bring us closer to comparable countries, and I am sure that this matter would be welcomed mainly by the Slovenian small economy, which represents 99% of the total Slovenian economy. The broad debate in the National Assembly in some way is in fact pointed out by Mr. Finance Minister Dr. Marble, already in the first sentence of his committee, which states, I quote: "I must also say that the aim of the Ministry of Finance is also to reduce the administrative burden which the economy in particular has on the economy as much as possible in the tax area and in this regard we have embarked on a specific action plan to reduce these administrative burdens." Ladies and gentlemen, I think the State Council has something to say here. We're all in the field. New Slovenia's Parliamentary Group – Christian Democrats make regular monthly field visits and listen to what the economy tells us in particular. We do not come up with solutions, but we listen to what the economy needs in order to actually better focus on its core business so that it can better develop new products, open new markets and so on, not deal with paperwork. This is a well-meaning proposal and, as the New Slovenia Group has said, will support this decision.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u294	Mr President, thank you for saying so. Dear colleagues and colleagues! I was not satisfied with the Minister's response. In the area of space regulation in Slovenia is a real anarchy. More than 100 municipalities do not have accepted municipal spatial plans to be adopted by 2009. The placement of state-of-the-art projects into space takes several years, including 10, 15 years, such as the second track, such as the motorway, the valley leg. Then we have over tens of thousands of blacksmiths, about 300,000 buildings built in non-compliance with regulations, we have long-term procedures for obtaining construction permits, which drives away mainly foreign investors, since the Czech Republic has five times more foreign investments than Slovenia. We have specific cases where that's why foreign investors went elsewhere. While the government also has the Spatial Planning Act and the Construction Act in its normative programme for 2015, nothing is yet being done as far as we know. However, the preparation of such laws is long-term if we want them to be good, so that we can correct everything that is delayed, that rules such as those of Western countries will be made available, that we will allow faster acquisition of building permits, placement in space, and also in due time prepare all necessary for foreign investors who are and are interested in investing in Slovenia. But with us, as if we don't want them, as if we don't need new jobs, we don't need money in the budget. Here we do not need money in preparing this legislation and adopting it, because we know we do not have it, but we do need expertise and will, so I appeal that we hold a debate in the National Assembly on this issue, which is essential for regulating the situation that we have in our country. If we do not do so, it is difficult to expect new investments from both our economy and foreigners, even though the Prime Minister of the Government has stated that we will do everything possible to have foreign investors come. So I ask you to confirm that we have a debate on this and speed it up.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u300	Thank you for your word. Although I have very little hope that this proposal will be put to the vote, let us try. We were aware that this country is not adequately provided for the treatment, training and education of children and adolescents with severe behavioral and emotional problems. These are children and adolescents who were the victims of domestic violence when they were young. There is no suitable accommodation for them in our health and social care system. It is too often that protective institutions or youth homes, despite a decision to install, refuse the admission of such children and, as a result, such children and adolescents may be treated in extreme cases even in protected wards of psychiatric hospitals, along with adult patients, which is unacceptable. The treatment of children with severe behavioural disorders has been maintained for at least 10 years, which constitutes a serious violation of children's rights. When these children and adolescents are 18 years of age, they are officially no longer a problem because they are full - grown. But neglecting this problem is an indescribable suffering that often ends with suicide. Many of them are also endangering their surroundings, including, unfortunately, juvenile murderers and full - time offenders of worse crimes. A lot of this suffering could be prevented without a lot of money. Therefore, although the Minister has promised to solve this problem, she would have asked for a more serious hearing, since the Ombudsman had already said six years ago that the State which should act had cancelled in this area. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u303	Mr Jan Mahnic has an explanation for the vote on his own behalf.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-03-03-SDZ7-Redna-05.ana.u304	Thank you, President. I will support my colleague Kordiš in his request for a debate, although I do have diamitral opposite views. I am in favour of raising the defence budget, which is why I think it is right to hold a debate. Quo exercises, we can say Slovenian defence policy. I think we haven't followed our defense doctrine in a long time. Slovenia will soon not even be able to guarantee those commitments and demands, the tasks that are in law in defence. We know that last year, at the NATO Summit in Wales, Slovenia pledged to give 2% of GDP for defence by 2020 and within these two percentage points to give 20% for investment. At present, investments are only 3% and we are on the very tail of the NATO states. I believe that the equipment provided by the Slovenian military is inadequate to update the equipment. Last but not least, we send our soldiers on a mission abroad, which is right, because only if our neighbouring countries are stable does that mean that we are safe. That is why I support these missions, and I think it is right that we are there. However, it is not necessary to finally discuss where this defence policy will go, how our commitments to NATO will go, how much we will give for defence, how much we will give for investment, especially, whether we will continue to lie to our colleagues about NATO, how we will meet these and those requirements, or how we will just tell them in their faces, "Look, guys, girls, we are not going to do this anymore. We'll give it up for defense as long as we can."
